Hi, Doctor. It's really nice to meet you, and thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I hope I'm in the right place for this... 

I guess I've just been having a little trouble keeping things together lately. It's probably nothing major, just normal stress, but... well, what finally pushed me to make an appointment is that I just can’t seem to organize all the moving parts of my life anymore. I have all these things going on—patent notes I need to review, workshop prep, gallery deadlines, team decisions at work—and instead of prioritizing them properly like a normal person, I just end up starting whatever is loudest. You know? Like, whatever is right in front of my face, instead of what actually matters. 

I just feel like I should be better at managing my schedule at my age. Does that make sense as a reason to be here?

I don't think I'm hyperactive or anything. I mean, I can sit through a meeting just fine. I try really hard to look professional and attentive, especially on those long design calls.

No, I definitely pause and think. I mean, as a software developer, you really can't just go around making snap decisions, right? You have to think about all the edge cases and how one change might break something else.

activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for things I need to work, like my USB-C adapters, stylus, sketchbook, or even my badge, and it can derail a whole morning because I have no memory manifestation: If Slack pings, a browser tab flashes, or even a visual idea pops into my head, I can lose the thread of what I was coding and spend twenty minutes following some unrelated design rabbit hole.\n- key: S012\n  name: Feeling_Restless\n  description: Often runs about or climbs in situations where it is inappropriate. Well, um... I guess it happens on those really long design calls. You know how those can drag on for hours, right? Anyone would get a little stiff sitting that long. So, yeah, I might feel a little... pressure, I guess? Like this physical need to just stretch my legs. 

So I might pace around my home office a bit, or, you know, just shift from my desk chair to the armchair in the corner. But I'm still totally paying attention! I always make sure I look focused if my camera is on. I don't want anyone thinking I'm not engaged in the meeting. 

When I'm not on a call... no, not really. I mean, I'm usually deep into writing code, so I'm just sitting at my desk working hard. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just wandering around all day instead of working. It's really just a way to stay alert during those marathon meetings.
